ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e3782	We are now arriving at the current hour on the subject Research creates jobs First, Mr President, Mr Novotny spoke. His speaking time is 10 minutes. The Federal Minister will then deliver an opinion on the subject. Please, Mr Novotny.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e3812	Mr President. High house! We have chosen as the theme of the current hour "Research creates jobs". Of course, the field of research has many aspects, but I would like to look at very specific research issues here from the point of view of economic policy and thus of employment. It has now become common good that research and, above all, development and technology are fundamental issues and one of the most important factors of production in the economy. In Austria there is a need for re-examination in this respect – that is to say, quite openly. We have, for example, a need for re-examination in the material field. Economic research is too low in Austria as a research quota. We also need follow-up in the spiritual field, because research in Austria is still seen with skepticism, with caution and as a danger, and not as an opportunity and not as a challenge, as I believe it would be more useful. Much has already happened in this direction. It's also getting a lot. This current hour will now provide an opportunity to provide information on this area. I would just like to add a few points which I think are particularly useful. One example is the K-plus competence centres. This is a direct link between research and economic activities with appropriate regional links. Examples are the microelectronics centre in Villach, the Light Metal Competence Center in Ranshofen, the software center in Hagenberg near Linz and the like. That is, I think there are already very concrete, very interesting approaches. Further point: It is important that we establish European research areas in Austria. As a concrete example, I should like to mention the wind tunnel, which has been built here in Vienna as the core of a world's leading competence centre in the rail vehicle sector. I can refer to the activities of the ERP fund. And I should like to draw attention to the greater possibilities that the research funding funds now have, not least because of increased funding from the Austrian National Bank's anniversary fund. Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to take advantage of today's day to point out that the tax reform, which has been designed and completed this week, contains very important points which are directly research-related. The first area is the increase in the research allowance from currently 12 and 18 percent to 25 and 35 percent respectively. This increase in the research allowance is also an important indication for entrepreneurial decision-making in order to give greater weight to research. I therefore believe that this tax reform is also a very important structural aspect. I would like to stress two other areas in the context of the tax reform, namely measures to improve professional qualifications. In the future, as for investments in real capital, there will also be opportunities for investment in human capital. We have provided for a 9 per cent Quasi investment exemption for this purpose. And what's important is: For the first time, vocational training activities of workers will now be able to be taken into account to a specific extent in tax terms. We believe that this is a very important step forward, because it is important for workers to be able to continue their training at a time of rapid technological development. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Another area associated with the tax reform is the promotion of new business start-ups – something that is particularly sensitive to technology policy. We have, in particular, provided for a significant reduction in additional labour costs, namely 7 percentage points, in the case of redundancies. I believe that this tax reform, which we will now present to the House, is an important concrete step in order to give more weight to research and development in Austria in economic reality. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, you have to say – I have already suggested this –: Of course, research funding is only an aspect of innovation policy, perhaps not even the most important. This means that innovation-oriented research policy must be considered very comprehensively. There are also issues of risk financing, for example. In this respect, we have also made significant progress in Austria. There are already venture capital financing by several credit companies. In this area, as I mean, we are in the right direction. In my opinion, a very serious area is administrative barriers. I recently had the opportunity to have a conversation with Austrians in Munich who are working there in the field of biotechnology. It is really a big problem that while there are a number of young people who have been highly trained at the Austrian universities, they have no way of applying their knowledge in Austria. One of the main problems is that there are a number of location barriers in Austria. I therefore believe that it is important for us to create the legal basis for strengthening the possibilities for industrial research. But I would like to say quite openly: One point that is of current sensitivity is the draft new plant law, which comes from Mr Umweltminister. If this new plant law comes in the way now envisaged, it is a massive obstacle to economic research in Austria from the point of view of the economic-related research community. I have to say quite clearly that this new law would do more harm in research, in technology-oriented research than we could do through the use of hundreds of millions of research funding. I mean, you have to see very clearly the corresponding dimensions. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! It must also be clear that research policy is international. There is an international competition of research sites. There is no research site in the world today that would be permanently secured. We must therefore also strive to be attractive in this competition. This is all the more important than, in the meantime, a number of important research companies, unfortunately, research-intensive companies are no longer in Austrian ownership. I am thinking, for example, of Immuno, one of the most important research sites in Austria, which, of course, had to compete in the internal competition within the group. It is important that we create the appropriate conditions here, not least as regards freedom of movement in the selection of research staff and administrative legal bases. I would like to mention another point in this context, which is also research-relevant: the issue of ownership of central research-related companies in Austria. For example, the role of ÖIAG as a core shareholder is not only an industrial policy question, but also a research policy question, because it is precisely about securing research skills – and these are just typical headquarter functions – for Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion: Winston Churchill has once said – this is unfortunately of special topicality today –: "War is a far too serious matter to leave it to the military!" – I would like to say in an analogy: Research policy is far too important to leave it in the narrower sense only to research politicians. It is a matter of general policy, and we wanted to underline this with this current hour.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e3901	The current hour is now under discussion by the Federal Minister. Its opinion should also not exceed 10 minutes. - Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e3923	Mr President. High house! For a developed industrial state like Austria, research is the basis for its further successful economic development. Research is the basis not only for demanding production, for demanding products, for demanding jobs, but is at the same time the basis for a competitive Austria. That is why this is a central point of government efforts. However, research also needs appropriate skilled workers to be able to make use of research. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Austria has so far been able to develop and maintain its position in Europe and in the world essentially over this extremely well-qualified workforce. They are the prerequisites that the effort that is being put into research can also be used. Let me say a few words about what we are doing in this sector and what is to be done in this sector. I believe that it is primarily a matter of creating an environment in which research and development can flourish. It is necessary, first and foremost, to ensure that there is a modernisation and opening up of the institutions which are state-funded or state-funded, because universities and non-university research institutions must be open to those who can make further practical use of these research results. We must ensure that universities that have a significant share of research in Austria continue to open up to society. We must ensure that the transfer of knowledge, of research results from the research institutes to the social exploitation phase is improved to the enterprises. We are doing this, among other things, through a whole series of measures. Mr Novotny has already pointed this out. The K-plus centres, the competence centres under the K-plus programme, are such an element in which we bring together high-quality research institutions with companies working in the very demanding field of research and development. This creates clusters from which new high-performance and competitive companies and research institutions can emerge. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] We are also doing this, for example, by setting up an impulse programme at the technical colleges, which we have already carried out last year and will be carrying out again this year. This programme deals with the transfer of knowledge from regional universities to the regional economy, in particular small and medium-sized enterprises. We are doing this by introducing a post-doc programme, in which the graduates of a doctoral programme are offered the opportunity to work together with small and medium-sized enterprises in a project, without the cost of doing research, developing work, and thus building a bridge from the smaller companies to the research centres. And we do it – to mention a final example in this list – by increasing the research allowance, but not only an increase in the tax reform, but at the same time creating a special 35 per cent exemption that rewards those companies that either increase their research expenses or even start to work in research and development. And in this segment we create very direct incentives for the employment of the most qualified researchers and developers in small and medium-sized enterprises. I mean, that's a real step towards more research in Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Of course, we also need – and this has also been the subject of the discussion that has been conducted with intensity and commitment in recent years – more money, both from the public hand and more money from the economy, from industry. But I think we can also look back on success in this area. For example, we have invested a lot of money in the so-called technology billions. We have put substantial funds into research from divestment proceeds – I only remember Telering’s proceeds – or also out of dividend payments received by the federal government as owners, and we will spend additional funds on sales proceeds – for example for mobile phone licenses – for research. High house! Each research schilling, which is issued in Austria, leads to about 20 sales schilling. We should be aware of this fact. It is worth investing in research! It is worth it for the state, but it is also worth it for companies. This 20 sales shilling, which is caused by a research schilling, is delivered to 80 percent in export. Austria can only hold its position in the world economically, industrially and in terms of development if we invest in research, if we are competitive in the world. We are on a good path in this respect. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] High house! Research funding, research policy, however, is not an instrument to achieve short-term success. On the one hand, research needs a solid foundation in the field of basic research and, on the other, does not benefit tomorrow. Basic research has, as it were, an indispensable advantage that it does not work without it. But when the concrete economic and industrial benefits will occur, it is not to be foreseen. Consider, for example, the development of laser technology, founded by Einstein in 1917, but it was only in 1958, that is, 40 years later, that it was first successful in bringing it into operation; the first industrial uses were only started in 1960. But then an incomparable boom began in the use of this new technology. What we must recognize in research policy is that it is worth investing in the foundations and that is exactly what is needed if we are to have solid success for work and industry in Austria afterwards. High house! In summary, I think we can say: We must first and foremost be aware that research-promoting policies mean that we must invest in people by training them optimally by doing everything we can to ensure that future workers get optimal training. We need to use all the gift reserves! It is not a question of leaving a part to the left, whether people who cannot afford higher-quality training today because of their income relationships, or women who are often disadvantaged in the education process. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] High house! We must also ensure the appropriate framework conditions for promoting and supporting curiosity and risk-taking among people. We need these qualities not only where research is concerned, but also where it is about people trusting themselves to set up a company to implement independently what they can. We need to promote and develop more indifference and risk-taking! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We are trying our best in the area of facilitating and supporting a better, better and successful transition from the very good training institutions to the companies. We must facilitate this transition, and we must improve it, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, by making it easier for them to take the first academic step, because this is often a necessary structural step in order to be able to take a foothold in the field of research and development. Finally, we must also ensure that, in the research promotion system, in communication, especially with small and medium-sized enterprises, understanding and information flow are improved. – There has been a great deal in this regard in recent years, and I think we can look and wait with confidence at the consequences of the policy that has now been launched. All these things are underway, and with the forthcoming "Research Strategy 99 plus", we will also ensure that the institutions working in the field of science and research are more focused on the output than on securing their position in the input of money. It is a question of ensuring that science and research are aware that technology policy is something that is being carried out in the interests of society, in the interests of the people of that country. That's what we're making policy for!

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e8874	We now come to points 5 and 6 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. We are entering the debate. The first speaker was Mr Dolinschek. 3 minutes of voluntary time limit. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e8905	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The report by the Ministry of Social Affairs on the activity of labour inspection in 1997 shows that the number of accidents and regulations on the protection of workers has continued to fall, that intensive information and advice is provided by the labour inspection, that safety awareness has increased in the establishments and that accident rates have fallen. 315 work inspectors were used to review 83 000 plants and construction sites in 1997. 15 000 support consultations have been carried out, more than in previous years. Of the total of 75 000 infringements found, some 65 000 were concerned with occupational safety and health protection, and about every 8th complaint concerned the employment of pregnant women and the rules on working time and work rest. In the case of steering controls, 5 900 infringements were also detected. Control, instruction and consultation are very important. They are important in road transport, air transport, banking supervision and in many other areas, as well as at work and at construction sites. We must, however, distinguish between bagatellas and gross excesses. There were also complaints in the construction, trade and in the restaurants, but there were minor setbacks in relation to the objections. However, it should be noted that three-quarters of all concerns in these sectors concerned safety and health protection. The number of accidents at work has also fallen slightly. However, the number of fatal accidents, which is very unfortunate, has increased in terms of construction, trade and metal production. In the construction sector in particular, there has been an increase from 41 fatal accidents in 1996 to 49. In 1996 there were 10 fatal accidents in metal production and in 1997 14. I believe that, in those areas which are particularly affected, priority should be given in the future to reduce the number of accidents, especially those which are fatal. According to this report, the decline in the number of complaints and infringements is due to intensive information and advice from labour inspections and increased self-responsibilities at the workplaces. But I mean, it is certainly also due to the fact that, even if there are reports of jubilation, not all accidents at work are reported; there is certainly a large number of dark cases in this respect. Fewer farms have been controlled and workers have been less affected by controls. Although 146,000 workers have been controlled, 170 000 workers were monitored in 1996. I think that we should not be overjoyed, but continue to work on the protection of workers and put the safety at work in the foreground.

Because it's still better, that's paid by the health insurance, that doesn't cost so much. "Normal" diseases are not as well paid as accident pensions or pensions due to occupational diseases. ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e10059	Mr. Minister! Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The professional law for lawyers and notaries was under the great principles of deregulation, liberalisation and, especially with lawyers, of new possible social formations. We have now made it possible for, in addition to the individual companies in which, of course, every person involved must have the qualification as a lawyer, also to be admitted to capital companies, namely GesmbHs. These have been allowed in Germany for a long time and will also be possible in Austria in the future. We have not clearly regulated – and we have not yet been able to do so – international cooperation, i.e. the question of what this will look like in the future if cross-border societies want to form or combine. On this issue, we did not want to make a regulation in this respect, because we want to make an international approach here. It makes no sense that Austria is already prejudicially pre-empting something and then perhaps colliding with our colleagues and with the standards in neighbouring countries, in particular those of the European Union. We want to open the gates to the law-based professions – and not to close them. The question of interdisciplinary cooperation, of course, was also closely associated with this debate. This means that not only lawyers should be able to form a society but, for example, other professional groups should be able to form companies together with lawyers. For example, the economic loyals have included in their law, which we decided last month, that such interdisciplinary cooperations are possible, but that the economic loyals must have the majority of voting rights and, of course, the majority must also be present as a dominant factor. If a similar provision were introduced for lawyers, i.e. the possibility of interdisciplinary cooperation on the condition that they have the majority, then both provisions would have been ad absurd, because there are no two majority. That is why we have also put this question back once again in order to explain under which voting rights regulations, under which dominant regulations and, above all, especially – and this seems to me to be the essential – which professional groups should be possible interdisciplinary cooperation. In fact, it is clear to everyone that lawyers should be associated with economic trustees or with business consultants in order to be able to act on the market as providers of a service. However, it is already to be questioned whether this should apply to lawyers and property trustees, lawyers and architects, lawyers and builders, or at all other companies or commercial firms, whether there are incompatibilities of classical nature and whether such cooperations still correspond to our understanding of liberal professions with their respective disciplinary law and their professional regulations. That is why we have reversed the solution to this question. However, as soon as we have clearer framework conditions and in particular see how our European neighbours deal with this question, we will continue to discuss them here in this House. The long duration of training for lawyers was also discussed. In particular, this training period is very long in European comparison. However, I would also like to make a commitment to quality from this point of view. Not only do we have the longest duration of training, but we also have one of the highest qualities in the legal professions in Europe – and this should not simply be ignored or given out easily. It is, however, worth remembering that there can be some kind of domestic discrimination, and that is why it is still necessary to solve this question. [[President Dr. Fischer shall take over the chairmanship.]] Let me say a last sentence on the ban on advertising. [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign and shows the speaker by a gesture the number one.]] Thank you. For a ban on advertising: It is true that the population often does not know what assistance and services lawyers and notaries offer because there is a quasi-advertising ban. So if you want to be a contemporary participant in the service market towards legal advice, then you will have to let this advertising ban be completely relaxed and allowed in the context of the allowed competition.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e10703	Mr President. High house! I would like to take the urgent question to the House to give full information on the results of Agenda 2000, i.e. on what has been achieved for Austria, for Austria's farmers, and also on the further course of the negotiations, in particular with regard to the final question today and tomorrow in Berlin. The Agenda 2000, as presented by the Commission – and this has made Austria clear from the outset – has never been rejected by us, but has been critically assessed where it is necessary. I believe that this very reasonable attitude, which Austria has taken from the outset, has also given us the opportunity in this negotiation to enforce what is necessary for Austrian farmers. The main focus of the negotiations was from the outset on the market regulation issue, particularly in the milk sector, and it was apparent that two major groups within the European Union were crystallizing during this negotiations in the milk sector: Those countries which have a liberal approach to agricultural market policy are, in particular, Great Britain, Sweden, Denmark or Italy, and those which are more committed to farming, of course, Austria. At the heart of these negotiations was the central question of reducing intervention prices, which Austria has previously assessed very critically, but I would like to say once again that intervention prices are so-called fixed prices, where they are necessary, for example in intervention. However, it is not a question of restricting market prices, but it must be the agricultural policy objective that the actual market prices, the proceeds received by farmers, will continue to be above intervention prices in the future. It was also a priority that we should continue to maintain control of quantities in the future in the key areas, such as the milk quota, such as set-aside in the cereals sector, because the control elements represent important elements of agricultural policy. And it is clear that the question of rural development from the outset plays a key role in the reconception of the European Union's agricultural policy. I also consider this rural development to be very central for Austria and the specific Austrian conditions. Ladies and gentlemen! The negotiations on Agenda 2000 have therefore succeeded in achieving a great deal for Austria, which is important for the Austrian farmers. A positive development has succeeded in bringing positive impetus to rural regions. It is clear that we have succeeded in preventing some serious deterioration. I would like to stress, however, that in spite of these measures that we have succeeded, the agenda and the reform of agricultural policy for Austrian agriculture will be a major challenge everywhere. It's not beautiful, it's quite clear. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Now on the individual questions. Question 1: The European model of agriculture, as adopted by the Ministers of Agriculture in November 1997, means to have a competitive agriculture, to have a multifunctional agriculture, to ensure sustainable land management and to ensure cross-border management also in the less favoured regions. These elements of Agenda 2000 are quite interesting when I think about rural development, for example, when I think about the milk quota scheme as a safeguard of the management possibility, when I think about environmental orientation, when I think about the measures in the mountain area, so that we can say that the compromise proposal now before us makes a contribution to ensuring the European model of agriculture. It is clear, however, that in the future the European Union needs constant effort to defend the European agricultural model in international competition. On questions 2, 3, 4 and 5, all of which concern rural development: For the Austrian Presidency, it was clear from the outset that rural development will play a key role as the second pillar of the European Union's common agricultural policy. Austria has therefore also dedicated this issue to the informal council in St. Wolfgang, which was a real success. On the basis of the Austrian preparatory work and on the basis of the negotiations of the Austrian Presidency, the Regulation on Rural Development has now been completed. The Regulation manifests and establishes the promotion of rural development by the EAGGF as the second pillar of the common agricultural policy. This rural regulation includes the previous accompanying measures on the agricultural market, as well as the new elements of rural development. Austria has dealt with this rural development in the context of the "Agricultural Future Austria" project in good time, so that we are in a position to be able to make the best use of it for Austria by the year 2000. Its main focus is on the long-term protection of agricultural environmental programmes – thus ensuring the solid basis for our Austrian environmental programme in the future – and the new mining promotion that will allow us to introduce the co-financed base amount for the smaller farms in the mountain areas from the year 2000 onwards – an important measure to preserve our rural structure. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ]] It has succeeded in eliminating discrimination against the auxiliary farmers in the field of investment promotion, because I felt it necessary from the Austrian structure to come to equal treatment here. There will also be sector plans for the processing industry in the future with regard to their competitiveness. There will be new project opportunities in the forestry sector, an important income level in our farm-structured agriculture and forestry, and it will – that is new – provide cooperation projects across rural areas and not just in 5b areas between agriculture, economy, tourism, gastronomy and commerce, which serve to strengthen the life force of rural areas. The financing of this rural development is ensured. About EUR 5 billion a year is available for this rural development. Co-financing is fixed 50 percent for environmental programmes, between 25 and 50 percent for all other programmes. On questions 6, 7, 8 and 9 concerning beef and veal market rules: The Austrian attitude in the negotiations was clear from the outset: We want as little price reduction as possible, we want control elements, we want intervention as a safety net also in the future, and we need to maintain our quotas, as negotiated in the Accession Treaty, and the inclusion of female cattle in the premium regime, in particular the question of calcin, which plays a key role in the mountain regions in the western part of Austria. The result: Intervention prices are not reduced by 30 percent as planned, but by 20 percent. [[Abg. Wenitsch: Bravo!]] It is possible to have a security intervention in the future. The premiums are increased for all animal categories. The suckler cow quota and the male cattle quota remain unchanged for Austria. It will improve the extension premium, which is important for our businesses in disadvantaged areas. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] There is a specific solution for Austria as regards the question of calcin in the mountain areas. We can use a share of the suckler cow quota for the specific promotion. A general slaughter premium is introduced for all animal categories in the bovine sector, i.e. for bulls, oxen, cows and calves as well as for slaughter calves – an important instrument for the control of quantities. On questions 10, 11, 12 and 13 concerning milk: For Austria and Austria's agriculture, milk is the key area, so from an Austrian point of view, the negotiating position was clear: safeguarding the milk quota also for the coming period; to a large extent the possibility of introducing an income compensation in the dairy sector in the future; take into account mountain areas in the area of milk production; Provisions for active milk producers and as little as possible reduction in prices in this area. The result: The milk quota will continue unchanged until 2006. A review will be carried out in 2003 on what will take place from 2006. In the field of reform, it is not as planned to implement from 2000 onwards, as it has succeeded in extending this deadline until 2003 and only then will this reform become effective. As proposed by the Commission, the 15 per cent price reduction will take place from 2003 onwards, but the income compensation for dairy farmers has been changed: It will not, as proposed, take place on the basis of the virtual cow, but on the basis of the milk quota. As a substitute for the planned and unrealised increase in the milk quota in the mountain areas, the expansion premium for dairy cows in the mountain areas was very important for the Austrian mountain farming industry. A linear increase in the milk quota will also occur for Austria by 1.5 percent from 2003 onwards, which is 41 000 tonnes. Furthermore, it has succeeded in imposing a specific Austrian requirement, namely the long-term demand for the conversion of unexploited D-Quots of 150 000 tonnes into supply quotas; an important and forward-looking scheme for the Austrian dairy industry. On questions 14, 15 and 16 concerning arable crops: I say immediately that the field of agricultural culture is within the scope of the agenda, where we really have to expect great problems. There's nothing to beautify. This is a difficult part of the overall compromise of Agenda 2000. As originally proposed, the price reduction will amount to 20 percent, but not at once, but in two stages. There will be opportunities to implement the set-aside in the future. In the first two years of reform, the set-aside rate will be 10 percent. There will be a uniform area premium in the cereals sector, which will be increased and available uniformly for all crops. There are exceptions: For protein plants, there is a supplement to this uniform crop scheme, as well as durum wheat. Oilseeds are reduced to the single area premium in three stages. There are some changes I think are important. It is true, for example, that the payment of premiums will not take place until the following year, but in the year in which the harvest takes place. In the end, this is also important for ensuring the payout points for our Austrian farmers. In the area of starch potato production, the premium is higher than originally proposed by the Commission. In return, the starch potato ratio is reduced to a small extent. In particular in the cereals sector, it is therefore important to give the answers already addressed from the Austrian perspective. This concerns, for example, the rapid approval of the ÖPUL 2000 environmental programme. I would like to inform you that, as a draft, ÖPUL 2000 is now under negotiation in Brussels, and the notification has been made by the Ministry of Agriculture. On questions 17 and 18 concerning wine market: In principle, I would like to say that the reform of the wine market system in the European Union has produced a very reasonable result; a very reasonable result in that the price discipline in European wine production remains fundamental. There is only a very small plantation possibility, which for Austria is about 700 hectares. There are new instruments in the wine market system for the conversion of varieties, for the cleaning of flora and for improved management techniques. This will support the necessary modernisation of the sector. In the wine sector, producer organisations and industry associations have a greater importance in the future than they have previously. The oenological procedures, i.e. the methods of wine preparation, remain unchanged. The status quo in wine önology remains. We can therefore continue to achieve our special quality with our traditional methods in the future. Important Austrian claims have been enforced in terms of designation law and trade with third countries. On question 19 concerning the cost of the reform: The Commission has calculated that the cost of the reform, as decided by the Ministers of Agriculture, will amount to some EUR 314 billion for the period 2000 to 2006. As a result, this financial volume is about 0.5 percent above the level initially proposed by the Commission at EUR 312 billion. There has been a debate on the degression in this context. Austria has introduced the model of the company-size degression as a clear alternative to the time degression. At the level of the agricultural ministers, a result was finally achieved, which means neither a temporary degression nor a degression according to farm sizes. Austria has stated in a Protocol: Should the debate on the temporary degression come to the table again of negotiations, Austria will, of course, put on the table the model of business-size modulation, i.e. the staggering according to operating sizes. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Wabl: When does it have a view? What time horizon for modulation?]] This attitude, ladies and gentlemen, is a position of the Austrian Federal Government and is therefore represented at all levels of negotiation by the respective members of the government. Question 20: I think the agenda, as decided by the Ministers of Agriculture, is a reasonable basis for the negotiations of the European Union in the context of the next WTO round. Why? Firstly, because this decision gives the agenda clarity about the European position. Europe speaks with one voice and not with several votes. Secondly, The agenda means a shift from intervention prices and export refunds to direct income transfers. Thirdly, The cost of export refunds – a very critical chapter in the WTO – will fall through the agenda. Under the horizontal regulation, it will be necessary to impose ecological requirements on market organisation premiums. It is also planned that rural development in the field of environmental standards or animal welfare standards should be "feducated" with them, so that these elements at the forthcoming WTO negotiations will allow the European Union to engage in offensive negotiations in the WTO and will not be pushed into the defensive by the negotiators, as would otherwise have been feared. A WTO success will only be possible for Europe if it is offensive and uniform to the WTO negotiators. On question 21: Priority on enlargement was given from the outset to ensuring the necessary funding. In my view, therefore, it is a crucial step that the agricultural guidelines are not disputed; the agricultural guidelines which will ensure the necessary financial framework in the future. In the event of an extension during this period, i.e. between 2000 and 2006, it is necessary to review the financial perspective and, if necessary, to make necessary adjustments. The only concrete decision under Agenda 2000 is that the so-called pre-accession aid will be financed for the applicant countries – in my view, an important step, because these funds in these countries will ultimately also be used to implement those standards which lead to more competitive justice. In the context of enlargement, I should like to refer once again to the Austrian model of farm size recession, which is a very important dimension for future design in the context of the European Union's wants with its agricultural structures – large farms. On the question 22: Austria continues to have the goal of anchoring the market rules for renewable raw materials in the field of agricultural crops. That was in the In the course of these negotiations, Agenda 2000 is not possible – in brackets: not yet. It is important to build on rural development the possibility of providing support for renewable raw materials, in particular in the investment part. However, we will continue this debate at European level, and the price package will allow Austria, together with other partner countries, to take a new initiative in this direction. They know that the Federal Government has set itself the goal of creating an addition of biodiesel to diesel oil – an important ecological measure. I can tell you that in the course of the political final negotiations on tax reform, up to 2% of rape methyl esters have been achieved if they are mixed with diesel to achieve mineral oil tax freedom – an important ecological facet. On the question of 23 which national measures are necessary: I said clearly: The results of the agenda require national answers because we want to continue the Austrian path of agriculture in the future. I see four major approaches to this: Firstly, further steps to relieve agricultural holdings. I would like to tell you - and that has already been said - that political clarity and agreement in the Federal Government is that the percentage of the tax rate for turnover-offaled farms will be increased from 10 to 12. This means 1.2 billion schilling for the Austrian farmers – an important step. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] I would like to give a second example: Mr Prammer and I have agreed that a novel of the Animal Health Act, which will be presented shortly, will replace the comprehensive investigation in some animal diseases with a sample investigation. Since we have a high standard of health, we can afford it. This change is a relief for farmers from research costs without endangering health. Second priority: Ensuring national co-financing. We need the clear signals given by the federal and state governments in the framework of the 40 billion package so that rural development can take place in an optimal way. Thirdly, We will present a rural programme. That means: Environment, ÖPUL 2000, base amount and all the other areas we wish to make offensive use of for the Austrian farmers. And fourthly, It must be clear that, given the outcome of the agenda, it is of central importance to continue to fight in a massive way for it, to take action and also to create the scope for the market position of the Austrian farmers – for example: Producer organisations – improve. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] On question 24 concerning timetable: As you know, today the European Council of Heads of State or Government and tomorrow the final negotiations on Agenda 2000 are taking place. I assume that the Heads of State or Government will succeed in presenting an overall outcome of Agenda 2000. It is clear that this overall result is only given if the agricultural part is included. The vast majority of the Member States have clearly stated that the content of the agricultural package does not make sense. I therefore assume that tomorrow we will have a result of the agenda. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e14228	Mr President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen of the House! With the two novels on the professional law of lawyers and notaries, there are now important legislative drafts to bring about far-reaching changes in the legal bases of these two classic legal professions. I would like to take the opportunity to point out from this occasion, as I would like to stress, the Minister of State responsible, the essential key point of view, which, especially in today's modern legal and economic life, also comes to the lawyers and notaries with a view to safeguarding and safeguarding the democratic rule of law, the fundamental and freedom rights of citizens and the good functioning of the legal system, which must also be said. Ladies and gentlemen! The liberal professions are – and one has to remember this over and over again – guarantees the enforcement of citizens' rights both to the state and its institutions and to other collective powers and overpowered economic enterprises. In our ever-increasing mass society, they guarantee individual citizens through personal advice, through personal help the necessary individuality and humanity in safeguarding their rights and interests. The increased international economic relations significantly expanded the field of activity of the liberal professions in the economic sector and assigned them new tasks. At the same time, the activity of the professions is also a prerequisite for the smooth functioning of these international economic relations and the associated economic opportunities of market participants. However, in the face of all openness and safeguards for internationalization, the legal profession exercise must not be overlooked, and I certainly do share the opinion of Mr Ofner, that legal advice and representation will continue to apply mainly to the local client through the legal council, the legal representation almost "ums eck", "in the side street". In order for the professions to be able to fulfil their important tasks now and in the future, certain framework conditions laid down in the statutory professional regulations need to be continuously adapted to changing requirements. If the liberal professions are made into far-reaching professional regulations, it should not be overlooked that many things have happened in recent years to modernise these professions and that they do not conclude themselves in any reasonable way with liberalisation and deregulation. However, liberalisation and deregulation must not lead to a change of identity in the core of the self-understanding of the professions, to a weakening of the basic principles applicable to the activities of the professions or to a loss of quality of their services to their clients. The trust of citizens in the services of the professions serving its fundamental interests, their independence, trustworthiness, confidentiality and a high quality standard of their professional services must be safeguarded. It should also be noted that the services of the liberal professions differ considerably from those of other service professions and cannot be measured by the standards of the commercial market alone and cannot be reduced to mere service facilities for industry and industry. In particular, in the interests of clients who are not generally able to assess quality and price adequacy of professional services, liberalisation and deregulation are set limits, and, so to speak, as a consumer protection measure, the necessary professional training rules and the control of their compliance are required. One of the basic principles of the professional service is independent and personal professional training. Their strengthening also contributes to a number of regulations in the two legislative drafts. Thus, the principle of personal training was taken into account when creating the new form of the GmbH, which undoubtedly strengthens the international competitiveness of the Austrian lawyer. Therefore, the otherwise permitted possibility of the appointment of foreign business leaders and the award of legal proceedings at the GmbH should be as excluded as – at least for the time being – a mere company and, at least, the participation of foreign capital companies. I do not think that this restriction will reduce the competitiveness of the law firms. Rather, I believe that this will strengthen the necessary close relationship of trust between lawyer and client. One aspect of the independence of the professions is to ensure the necessary economic basis for professional training. In order to maintain these, it is also necessary to ensure that legal and notarial benefits are adequately honoured, which, of course, must also be taken into account in social matters. This basic idea has also been taken into account in the changes made in the draft law on the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of the Law of Officials of the European Communities. Another indispensable basis for the activities of the liberal professions is the absolute seriousness and confidence of citizens in the integrity of the legal and notarial profession. In this respect, the legal basis provided for in the Rules of Procedure for the Directives of the Austrian Bar Association on the exercise of trusts and the legal provisions laid down in the Notarial Regulations should be seen in relation to the obligations of the Notary in connection with trusts and the electronic trust register laid down so far by directives. The significant increase in the minimum amount of insurance for professional liability insurance in the legal and notarial order primarily serves the legitimate interests of the clients, but also provides some protection of the lawyer and notary for economic disasters. Another essential feature for the freedom of the legal profession and its professional independence is the democratically legitimated self-government of the profession, which has already been addressed by Mr Krüger, with the power of regulation, control and discipline. It is an efficient judicial system, which ensures that not a few black sheep can call into question the reputation of an entire profession, that is not only in the interests of the profession, but also in the interests of the clients and therefore indispensable condition for a general acceptance of this professional self-government. In this sense, the additional possibilities provided for in the Disciplinary Statute for lawyers and lawyers, in particular the new measure for the protection of foreign property entrusted to them, are to be seen. In order to enable the professions to live up to their responsible activities, it is more necessary than ever to have a high professional quality standard, which can only be achieved through comprehensive vocational training. It is precisely because I am aware that there are major differences in vocational training across Europe, but, as I believe, there is also a need for a certain degree of harmonisation of the training standards of the professions in the European Union with regard to freedom of services and establishment in the European Union, that I have also made this issue the subject of a conference organised during the Austrian EU Presidency in Vienna on "The importance of the professions in the integrated Europe" and I hope that this event will bear the appropriate fruit. At any rate, I consider it incompatible with the requirement of the high quality standards of the liberal professions, overhaste and unreflected levelling down and, as often required, to speak at the lowest level as possible, for example, to shorten the respective training periods inappropriately or even to eliminate professional examinations. Optimal training and training of professional candidates are the best guarantees for the expertise needed in the interests of clients and in the interests of their own career and competitiveness. As far as Mr Graf's position is concerned, and in particular their social security, I understand this point in principle and believe that it is necessary to deal with it more closely, with the improvement of the provision of services provided by the novel. As for the legal advertising you have referred to, Mr Krüger, I trust the commitment of the Austrian Bar Association to the fact that the relevant provisions of the directive are further liberalised in line with the provisions of the law against unfair competition. High house! I am sure that, with the two draft laws in place today for the adoption of the decision, we will take an essential step towards the rational modernisation of the professional rights of lawyers and notaries, which will enable these two professions to fully fulfil their responsibility for the democratic rule of law and for the individual citizens in the future. I would therefore ask you to approve these two proposals. Thank you.

But where it is found that criminal acts have been committed, there are currently gaps because the criminal provisions are either non-existent or different and therefore a persecution is not possible. These gaps are aimed at closing the two proposals, namely the Convention on the control of bribery and corruption, which is now continuing, for all OECD countries, and the Convention on the Protection of the European Union’s Financial Interests in the field of the European Union. It is now essentially a matter of standardising minimum criminal offences in a uniform manner, ensuring that there is also a procedural aid, and that, if necessary, extradition can take place. ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e15618	Mr President. Minister! High house! One of the most important and, as I believe, the most important tasks of policy is to promote the best possible protection of children worldwide. Each child should have the right to grow up "to full and harmonious development of his personality in a family and surrounded by happiness, love and understanding". – This is what the Preamble of the Convention says. And by the way, it says: Priority should be given to appropriate measures to enable the child to remain in the country of origin. – That is, priority over adoption are given to all those measures that improve the situation of the child in the home country, the home country. If no suitable family can be found for a child in the country of origin, in the home country, an international adoption for the child can offer the advantage of coming to a permanent family. However important the possibility of an international adoption for a single fate may be, it is not, in my view, a substitute for appropriate social policy measures in the countries of origin. This Convention also lays down that international adoptions should be carried out for the benefit of the child, but also in respect of the fundamental rights of the child. The central point of this Convention for international adoptions is therefore the explicit anchoring of the child's well-being, which is the task of every adoption procedure. Absolutely to prevent child abduction, trafficking with children and the sale of children. I think that trafficking in children is one of the worst and worst phenomena we encounter today. That is why I support the ratification because I think that this agreement is an instrument which regulates international adoption and will not be abused for the purpose of child trafficking. The interest in international adoptions has increased enormously in recent years, but at the same time, abuse has increased. In rich countries, the willingness to adopt children from poorer countries is greater and the so-called free market is regulated for supply and demand, but this also promotes the increase in abuse. Private associations, religious organizations and lawyers make themselves worldwide intermediaries of adoptions abroad, which often do not have in mind the child's well-being, but have the goal of dealing with the need and the predictable compassion. We must oppose this phenomenon with all the appropriate means, with all the means available to us, because there must be no child trafficking, and there must be no child abduction. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Consequently, Article 4 of the Convention explicitly states that consent to adoption must not be caused by a payment or other consideration, and Article 32 expressly prohibits any undue profit in connection with the mediation and settlement of international adoptions. However, I also consider the protection rules of the Convention to be very important. I also think it is important that international cooperation is ensured by the authorities of the home and host countries. The central responsibility of the authorities is also ensured, as there was a real regulatory deficit in this respect. That means that it was also high time to stop delaying ratification. I think it is equally important that The child is well given the highest importance, making it clear that the interests of other persons must also be taken into account, for example, the rights of biological parents and the intended adoptive parents. It is therefore necessary to strike a balance between the interests of all the persons involved. International adoptions can and should, if they are implemented in accordance with the standards of this Convention, result in children coming from a most unfortunate situation, from hopeless situations of life to a safe environment. But everything is to be done to completely prevent any abuse. For this purpose, this Convention offers a suitable instrument, and that is why my group is supporting in the interests of the children of this Convention.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e16423	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! On the occasion of the insolvency innovation, we have initiated the fact that the rules on mass remuneration which have been in force until now or to this day are more transparent and, above all, uniformly regulated throughout Austria. I was also concerned with the suggestion to this new law that the masses should be strengthened and in insolvency proceedings not only sought that the mass administrators be adequately rewarded and then nothing left for the masses. It has also been shown in the preparation for this novel that there are serious differences throughout Austria. In part, judges have privileged their remuneration to the mass administrators. These differences throughout Austria have now been eliminated and a uniform and at the same time transparent line has been created, which – I am particularly pleased – has been designed in a performance-oriented way. There will be a regular reward, and I would like to say from here that the legislator imagines that this standard wage is actually going to be the rule, and that it is only in very exceptional cases that there will be surcharges and discounts, especially if it is really shown that the mass manager is particularly intensively seeking to improve the volume of mass, or in other cases, if the mass manager is not really profitable. We have also made an amendment to the Government's proposal in the Committee by making it clear that it seems to us to be important if special mass exploitation is better rewarded if it is freely granted and not only done through the court. That is to say, the judicial recovery has so far led to a lower reward than the other types of recovery. I would like to make it clear here that this can only apply and only if the mass manager actually acquires earnings for the exploitation of special masses. So, if the special-mass beneficiary comes to mind – just think of the pledge right of a bank registered in the ground book! – for example, a bank, even trying to recover and the mass administrator has no effort at all, then of course he should not get this higher reward. Therefore, the rules on surcharges and discounts apply as well. In the run-up to the Committee's deliberations and today's decisions, the creditor protection associations have, of course, also intervened particularly intensively, because they are also affected by the new regulation. That is, even what creditor protection associations are getting privileged at the expense of the masses, is now uniformly regulated throughout Austria and subject to a uniform view. It wasn't like that so far. This means that creditor protection associations in certain district councils may have been particularly favourable and in others less favourable. We have also agreed on 20 percent of the mass management remuneration as a basis of assessment for creditor protection associations. I know that, in principle, one can discuss whether such an association should at all be given privileged money from the masses if, anyway, it requires a fee from the creditors. However, we consider it to be a support from the Court for greater efficiency in the insolvency proceedings if creditor protection organisations seek to ensure that, in the insolvency proceedings, there is a uniform form of opinion regarding all the conflicting interests of creditors. That is why it was also natural for us to try to ensure that the remuneration of creditors' associations is as fair as possible. Since both the main groups which have intervened massively with us politicians are very dissatisfied with the legislation and have asserted conflicting interests, the present law in the middle found a compromise with which both groups are satisfied, I believe that we can decide this compromise as adequate. I think it is just that a fixed amount will be given to creditor protection associations in advance and that the additional amounts will be allocated per capita. The fact that we have not been able to decide to pay the creditor protection associations at the level of their claims has to do with the fact that the large claims in a bankruptcy are not supposed to exceed the small medium-sized suppliers so to speak. In addition, the thought of creditor protection organisations is not the enforcement of the individual claim, but the financing of a basic structure so that these associations can carry out their work for the benefit of the courts and for the benefit of the masses as a whole. As far as the company's book law is concerned, it is my concern to point out that we no longer have modern standards with regard to the publicity of the balance sheets. It is undisputed that we confess to the publicity that the deposit of balance sheets is a positive instrument. But it is not really to be seen why small businesses, i.e. small GmbHs, have to turn an advertisement on it, nor in the "Wiener Zeitung", which is not really read in the federal states. [[Abg. Haigermoser: There is today a motion for a resolution from us!]] It is also not practical to believe that someone will pick up these ads on the side of the page and then start to browse at some point to find out if the company concerned wants to know whether the balance sheet is stored – yes or no – since sometime it has been advertised. Unfortunately, we are also bound by EU law, which still prescribes the publication in a printing medium. I would therefore ask the Minister to press ahead with this at EU level with the realization that our electronic registers, such as the company book, developed in Austria, guarantee the publicity we need in order to protect creditors. If the EU joins us on this issue of our position, we would have to debureautize quickly and relieve businesses of costs. [[Abg. [[Abg. Haigermoser: Ms. Fekter is the first "government opposition speaker"!]]

The present draft law on a change to the company's book law is a timely step towards improving quality and improving efficiency. A large number of companies are registered with the company's book courts and tax authorities, whose indebtedness is evident and to which delivery is not possible. This results in an unnecessaryly high amount of work. By recasting the Amtslöschungsgesetz and transferring it into the Company Book Act, the official cancellation of non-profit societies is to be made more efficient and at the same time a certain legal adjustment is achieved. Since the law governing the dissolution and cancellation of societies and cooperatives dates back to October 1934, i.e. a German law, and was introduced in Austria in 1938, the step towards a new regime is essential. At this point, it should also be noted that other laws of this time are in force and need to be adapted and revised today. Before the cancellation, the representatives of interest and the tax authority responsible for the company's registered office shall be heard. The courts and tax authorities shall each other be required to provide information necessary for the implementation of this federal law. If, after the deletion, the existence of assets subject to the distribution becomes apparent, the settlement shall take place, with the controllers being appointed by the court at the request of a party. As the need for administrative simplification, an increase in efficiency, and also the need for legibility of laws are repeatedly highlighted, this federal law is an important contribution to the achievement of the objectives I have quoted. In all the confessions to the modern design of the company's book, taking into account the technical development, the use of modern media such as the Internet, our main objective cannot be the weakening of the Vienna newspaper, as it was apparent at the last committee meeting. However, I would also like to take the opportunity to thank you, ladies and gentlemen, very much, Mr Federal Minister, for the work done by your officials. ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e16668	The Federal Minister for Justice Dr. Michalek has now been reported to speak. - Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e16689	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The present insolvency management remuneration law is the last step in a gradual renewal of insolvency rights in recent years, which began with the introduction of the private bankruptcy by the bankruptcy novels in 1993 and continued with the development of company insolvency rights through the insolvency law laws 1994 and 1997. The aim of the new regulation is to bring it together again to create an Austrian-wide uniform, performance-oriented remuneration of insolvency administrators and privileged creditor protection associations. It should be transparent and comprehensible for all parties involved, i.e. courts, insolvency administrators, creditors' associations, but above all for creditors. The new regulation builds on the principles of previous case law. The mass manager is expected to receive a degressive percentage of recovery income in the bankruptcy or compensation requirement. In setting the percentages and the degression levels, an attempt was made to find a mean between the remunerations granted by the individual courts. In special exceptional cases, taking into account the circumstances of this individual case, a deviation may be made from the rule remuneration on the basis of certain criteria up and down. These principles should also apply to the reward of creditors' associations and their activities, not as representatives of creditors, but to those activities they provide in the interests of all creditors and in support of the court. You will receive a percentage of the remuneration of the insolvency administrator, as is the practice so far. In determining these percentages and in the division among creditor protection associations, we have taken a route of funding between the courts' partly quite different calculations and distribution methods. This is also a mediating solution between the models that are preferred by the two currently existing privileged creditor protection associations. With the rules on the remuneration of insolvency managers and the reward of creditor protection associations, we enter new land. The Federal Ministry of Justice will therefore pay particular attention to the development of the implementation of the new right. As to the claims of a privileged creditor protection association in the representation of entitled persons according to the insolvency insolvency insurance law falling within the competence of the Federal Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs, from the point of view of my department, I would just like to point out that the payments in question do not go at the expense of bankruptcy or compensation. The revision and updating of the Amtlöschungsgesetz in 1934 on the occasion of its integration into the Company Book Act takes into account the fact that in the Austrian company book courts a variety – Mrs Stoisits, I emphasize: a variety! – are registered by companies, especially companies with limited liability, which are ineffective and largely no longer exist in de facto at all. Not least because of the difficulties in the delivery of judicial documents, this causes great unnecessary effort, and the deletion of such companies is thus not only a question of the reliability and clarity of the company's book in the interests of users, but also a question of labour economy and the efficiency of judicial activity. The time for our project is favourable in so far as, I admit, the disclosure obligations extended by the EU Company Law Change Act for capital companies have become so to speak, in the course of the previous year. The qualified non-fulfilment of the resulting obligations will be the point of departure of such companies, in so far as they do not have obvious assets, without too much effort. The fears expressed by you, but also by Mr Krüger, that the deletion could be done too quickly, that there could be no ineffective societies "overrun" as well as the erasure process, I do not share at all, and I do not think they are a reason for rejecting this law. It is not true that the removal of a mere two-year inaction, a failure to comply with the rules by the company or an inaction of the court. According to Article 283 et seq. of the Commercial Code, the Court of First Instance does not simply accept the non-subsidy of the annual accounts, but must initiate and operate the forced criminal proceedings against society there, and in this context it will also receive further knowledge of the actual circumstances of the company. According to § 18 of the Company Book Act, the court must also try to get in touch with the company and draw attention to the possible cancellations before it intervenes in its company book rights. Furthermore, according to the new § 40 para. 2 Company book law to deal with the relevant representation of interest and the tax authority, and they will also say that the company is not mature if there are assets there. Finally, it is – and this is the most important thing – if there is evidence of a property – that can also result from an incompletely presented annual report, ladies and gentlemen, from the outset not to initiate the erasure procedure. All of this, I believe, is sufficient to ensure that only the so-called cartels, which in fact no longer have any operational activity and have no assets, will be affected by the erasure procedure. If, in fact, a non-profit society is affected, it can easily be re-entered, especially since it has not ceased to exist due to the deletion, but is only dissolved. All in all, I believe that the redefinition of the former Amtslöschungsgesetz will help to increase the transparency and clarity of the Austrian company's book and that we will also make a small contribution to the legalisation of the law, which is also to be welcomed with regard to the major activities of legalisation. Let me say a few words about the resolution, which is in the opposite context of the debate. The request made to me constitutes a confirmation and substantial support of our efforts in this regard. For example, a group of experts, in close cooperation with the Board of Economic Trustees, is working on a project to enable the electronic transmission of annual accounts to the company's book. The electronic submission to the Court of First Instance would, of course, significantly facilitate the electronic publication of the relevant data with significant savings for the undertakings concerned. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In this respect, the work will be completed by the end of the year. In addition, I would like to mention that our efforts to adapt the EU's publicity directive to the new technical possibilities of positive inclusion in the European Union at expert level can be expected to ensure that our ideas, with which Austria has a certain pioneering role, can be achieved in the foreseeable future. The registration of the submission of the annual accounts no longer needs to be published, because this can also be consulted electronically in the base book. In this sense, I hope that I will be able to report to the House in the foreseeable future on positive results within the meaning of the resolution. – Thank you for the attention.

This serves for transparency: transparency for the judge in terms of what the mass administrator is actually doing, but also transparency for the creditors, who, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, often did not know why, in the end, nothing has been distributed, but the mass administrator has received a considerable fee. Overall, I believe, the proposal is well suited to eliminate the previously unsatisfactory situation as regards the remuneration of mass managers and to achieve a higher efficiency of insolvency proceedings. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e16945	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! This genuine maladministration is also made clear by the fact that the pay practice is often differentiated from court to court, and that it is not possible to ensure clarity or to make the orientation of the service provided comprehensible. High house! The present draft law provides for transparency and performance incentives, first of all through a regular remuneration, which is a certain percentage of the recovery income or the compensation requirement achieved, second, by binding the judicial discretion on performance criteria and thirdly by the cost estimate obligation of the mass manager in carrying out business. Ladies and gentlemen! So far, damage has often been caused by the need to make decisions on closure or continuation of the insolvency without any comprehensible economic analysis. The new regulation now obliges the mass administrator to demonstrate clearly its recommendations for the appropriate economic strategy, with an explicit reference to the market, enterprise and financial situation. ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e17555	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! The present amendment to the Banking Act is essentially an adaptation to an EU directive which corresponds to the system of deposit protection and investor compensation. This transforms this system. Investments of up to 20 000 euros are thus secured in the event of insolvency. Some aspects of consumer protection are thus taken into account. It has also been possible, from the government template, to introduce a number of additional elements into the law, particularly as regards the period to which a security device must be claimed and until which a notification is required. This period has been extended from 5 months to 1 year. It also provides for an improved obligation to provide information for consumers in the field of security facilities. This information on protective devices must be provided in the warehouse. It is also necessary to provide written information concerning the institution which takes over this protective device, the liability and scope of the cover. Furthermore, we still wish – but this can be realized in the next novel – that these elements also become contractual content and are not only subject to suspension. I think that this could create a further substantial improvement. The Securities Supervisory Act is also essentially an adaptation to EU standards with regard to collateral. Significant improvements are being made to small asset advisors. For the small asset advisors, it is planned to waive the tested balance sheet. It is no longer necessary to demonstrate the main profession of main activity. Furthermore, the time limit for obtaining the commercial right is extended. I believe that this will be a major step forward. As Mr Firlinger has already addressed the topic of consumer protection – asset consultants, I would like to say the following: From our point of view, the main problem is not the small asset consultants, but the large structural distributions that are very often working on the edge of the legality or even beyond the edge of the legality. The small asset consultants and consumer protectionists are sitting in a common boat, and it must be our common concern that structural distributions do not work with people who do not have a concession and would not be able to obtain a concession at all. This leads, on the one hand, to a competition that the small asset advisors rightly feel is unfair, and it is also harmful from the point of view of consumer protection. We have tried to discuss this, and this has resulted in an amendment which does not go as far as we have discussed in the Committee's deliberations, but which is a very big step in the right direction. In principle, it is a question of being irrelevant to third parties whether the person acting for a large service undertaking is an employee or a worker, because in principle, the legal status of a worker's treatment is the same. I know very well that later, very often, there are so-called differences of opinion as to how detailed an information was in the consultation. I therefore believe that, on the basis of such standardised information on the key elements of a paper, which the investor receives and in which the income prospects, the risk, the maturity, liquidity and also the costs are cited, one could very well clarify such differences of opinion about good or bad advice afterwards. I would therefore also like to be a member of a money institution to be offered such a service, which would be possible on a voluntary basis at any time, in the future. I would also like to expressly welcome this amendment from the point of view of consumer protection. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e17992	Mr President. High house! A functioning capital market, which in particular allows companies access to equity, allows them to be more secure in crises, but also to really exploit the growth that can be achieved in an economy, is undoubtedly of crucial importance for an economy. It is therefore of crucial importance for employment security in our country. At the same time, however, there is no question that consumer protection must be given high importance and that this capital market must therefore function fairly, not only in the interests of businesses and employees, but also in the sense of investors who make their money available to the economy through this capital market. I am therefore very glad that this law implements the EU directive in this matter, which was adopted in 1997. We have asked the Constitutional Service whether entry into force is acceptable in this case for the past, and the Constitutional Service has, after a very in-depth examination of this matter, recognised that this path is quite feasible. That is why we made this proposal. I am therefore very glad that this law, which also brings consumer protection a further step forward, is being discussed here today and is now in the process of decision-making. Thank you.
