ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e19850	We are now negotiating points 18 to 23 of the agenda. The debate on this is being held under one. Since there is no wish for oral reporting, we are going into the debate. The first speaker is Mr Trattner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e19877	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! These are three grants for development aid or capital participation in the African Development Bank. Overall, it is a volume of 834 million schilling, one part of which has to be paid directly; the other part is the acceptance of liability. The direct payments are broken down as follows. Firstly, the Global Environment Facility must pay a total of 231 million schilling in the funds – the GEF 2 funds – in five annual instalments from 2001 to 2005. These are one-off payments for which there are no repayments. This will address more environmental promotions for climate change, biodiversity, international waters, ozone and the like. The second contribution goes to the World Bank Consultative Group on International Agricultural Research for the years 1999 to 2001. It is about an order of 51.9 million schilling, payable in three rates at 17.3 million schilling each. It is to be said that this agricultural research is basically correct, as it has brought a significant increase in output in the size of 80 per cent in the developing countries over the last decades. This is a great success, and this is certainly also due to this international agricultural research. However, one must also be aware that the population will increase by 90 million per year in the coming years and that by 2025 a total population increase of 2.5 billion is expected. On the other hand, land resources in developing countries are largely exhausted. This agricultural research is also no longer limited to developing countries, but should also be extended to industrialized countries. To this end, appropriate measures will be taken. The third contribution concerns the general increase in the capital of the African Development Bank. The total is 3,780 shares, 227 of which are payable and 3 553 are shares of which are capital. It is basically a question of paying the affordable shares in the order of 33.1 million schilling. These three development grants and capital investments are also concerned with the need for greater control of the purpose of use in the future. Secondly, we must ensure that all countries participate in this. For example, it is not a question of the United States taking the view that it is no longer or only to a lesser extent involved in this action, and that other States such as Germany, France or Italy will find any excuses as the Americans do not pay, so that there are financial gaps that need to be filled up again. If it is a contribution of solidarity – as such we see it, but critically – then all countries must also take part in the same framework. However, because this is still not secured and because a proper purpose of use has still not been demonstrated by the various control bodies, we shall not give consent to these three government proposals.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e19930	The next speaker is Mr Gusenbauer. Voluntary speaking time limit: 6 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e19951	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! In all the shorts required: Although each of these proposals would be worthy of more detailed consideration, I would like to limit myself essentially to three comments. Point one: As far as the issue of the increase in the capital of the African Development Bank is concerned, we consider this to be a useful measure, because after the African Development Bank crisis three to four years ago, on the one hand, the rehabilitation measures have been successfully implemented and, on the other hand, there is a positive economic growth potential in a number of African countries, which is also not uninteresting for a number of Austrian companies, as has become apparent in the light of the visit of the President of the African Development Bank. This does not mean, however, that the continent as a whole has prosperous economic prospects as long as there are a number of military conflicts in individual countries. In all the dramas of individual military conflicts, however, it should not be overlooked that individual countries have growth rates that are quite unique worldwide. For a country like Austria, it would not be appropriate to exclude itself from these growth prospects. That is why we believe that the scope of this African Development Bank for action should be extended, and that it should be useful for these possibilities to be used by Austria as well. [[President Dr Neisser shall take over the chairmanship.]] Secondly, We have a number of investment protection agreements to conclude, with the respect for certain criteria being very important to varying degrees: On the one hand, the investment protection agreements are necessary in order to advance trade and investment accordingly. On the other hand, these investment protection agreements must not open a back door, which will lead to unwanted immigration of foreign labour into the Austrian labour market. That is why we have made a point in the Committee's report, which I think is essential, namely that the investment protection agreements do not mean that we should be able to interpret measures which would go beyond the already entered into GATT or WTO commitments. I consider that, in the sense of the solidity of the Austrian labour market, it is necessary to supplement it; with this note, these investment protection agreements are a useful matter. I do not want to go into detail about the increases in individual funds located at the United Nations, such as the Global Environment Facility Trust Fund. I would just like to point out that this is one of the most successful funds, perhaps one of the most important remains of the Rio Conference on the fight against international greenhouse effects. And it is important that we support it. From the point of view of the Austrian taxpayer, it is to be said that the capital increase we are now carrying out is below our quota, i.e. it is an extremely economical measure. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e20089	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! As we are now discussing some investment protection agreements, I would like to point out that, as I believe, it is very effective that there has been unanimity in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy. Why? . Because it is important that we in principle recognise that such investment protection agreements support or contribute to the promotion of mutual economic contacts and that the greater the protection of investment is the easier the promotion of such economic cooperation between Contracting States. The reason why I am commenting on this is that such agreements as those relating to cooperation between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Croatia - I have already stressed this in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs - must also be pursued in the future. However, I would like to stress very well that in the past this did not in all cases lead to the mutual advantage. I have also mentioned in the Committee the specific case - without mentioning the name now - of a company to which I have the documents in which a dispute has been taking place for years. That is why I very much welcome the fact that all the political groups voted in favour of a committee, which says that it is expected that Croatia's bilateral disputes will be brought to a solution as soon as possible. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! It is not a question of trying to decide on new forms of investment protection agreements, without really insisting on the fact that previous cases are also resolved to the satisfaction of those Austrian companies that have invested millions there. Helmut Peter and comrades concerning a federal law amending the National Bank Act 1984 (1012/A)
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e20304	We're coming to the 24th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. The applicant shall first speak. – Mr Mag. Peter! You have to get rid of Mrs. Konrad's charm now, because you have to speak from the speaker's desk! I now give you the floor as the first speaker.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-24-020-XX-NRSITZ-00162_d7e20331	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I can draw your attention to a request from the Liberal Forum, which deals with the National Bank Act. They know that the Austrian National Bank Act in conjunction with the Austrian Banking Act provides that the institutions of the Austrian National Bank have a very important role to play in the control of the Austrian banking system and that the Federal Ministry of Finance is also required to provide information. The Oesterreichische National Bank's institutions, in particular the Executive Board, naturally know the economic situation and also the details of the economic situation of the Austrian banking system. The General Council as Supervisory Board of the Austrian National Bank is filled with high-ranking personalities. And there must be a reason for the existing National Bank Act to normize that only four members of the General Council may belong mainly to the administration of credit institutions. ==References== The appointment of Director-General Herbert Schimetschek as Vice-President of the General Council took place on September. Ladies and gentlemen! Give me the opportunity to make it clear here that I think Mr Schimetschek is an excellent personality of Austrian economic life, which is, of course, a profit for the General Council. However, the appointment as Vice-President is a smooth mistake! Ladies and gentlemen! If one belongs to the General Council as a member of the Office of the Austrian National Bank, according to the Rules of Procedure of the Austrian National Bank, one takes part in the Executive Board meetings, and at these Executive Board meetings, the Vice-President even has to hold the Presidency in the absence of the President. He thus has all the information available today on the Austrian credit instrument in the field of the Austrian National Bank and also the Ministry of Finance, because there is a mutual obligation to provide information. If the same personality is now also Chairman of the Supervisory Board of the First Austrian Sparkasse, it obviously knows the economic situation and economic situation of a Bank Austria, a Raiffeisenzentralbank et cetera. Ladies and gentlemen! This is highly incompatible and an error in the law that we want to eliminate by this request. It is not possible for a person from economic life to forget the information she received at the meeting of the Board of Directors of the Austrian National Bank once again because she then serves as Chairman of the Supervisory Board of the First Austrian Savings Bank. Let me quote from § 33 of the Austrian Stock Act. That's what it's called under lit. c "Interest collision" literally: "As a result of the foreign interest commitment, there can be a wide range of conflicts of interest. The careful exercise of the Supervisory Board member's duties is to avoid conflicts of interest or, if they are inevitable and become a conflict of obligations, to resolve the business interest in accordance with his primary obligation." It also refers to the conflict of obligations: "In such a case, the Supervisory Board member cannot relieve himself of the argument that the fulfilment of obligations vis-à-vis one company justifys the breach of obligations towards the other. In order to resolve the conflict, the Supervisory Board member must give up one of the two offices." I continue to quote from Reich-Rohrwig's comment, also on the question of organic fidelity and conflicts of interest – literal quotation –: "In addition, it must be assumed that the Supervisory Board member must always give priority to the corporate interest. If no other solution is found, one of the colliding offices must, if the conflict of interest becomes a conflict of interest, settle down." – I could quote other experts from legal literature. That means: If Director-General Heribert Schimetschek does not use his information as a member of the Executive Board of the National Bank, to which he belongs as Vice-President, as Chairman of the Supervisory Board of the First Austrian Sparkasse, he has committed a gross breach of duty. However, if he uses this information, he in turn violates the obligation of confidentiality in accordance with the National Bank Act. In my opinion, therefore, it is a very clear conflict of obligations, and I regret that Mr Bundeskanzler Mag. Climate, although I did it on 4 May. On September 8th, this conflict of interest and conflict of obligations had been pointed out by the order on September 8th. On September 11th and on my letter, unfortunately, I was only made on September 11th. In December, the Commission sent a reply to the questions referred for a preliminary ruling to the Court for a preliminary ruling. What does this mean for us, ladies and gentlemen of the House? . I believe that we must make a change in the National Bank Act by removing a single word. In § 22 para. The third sentence of the old version reads as follows: "No more than four members of the General Council shall be members of the Executive Board of credit institutions." – This word "main professional" must be deleted, because, as I have just shown you by means of the cited law bodies and the comment, a president of a Supervisory Board is of course obliged to make all his expertise and knowledge available to the company. Because otherwise he also comes into the liability of the Supervisory Board. On the other hand, no one can expect him to use the knowledge he has received as Vice-President of the General Council and thus a member of the Board of Directors on other institutions, and what he has received in terms of competition information. No one can keep it apart! I therefore propose that we make the amendment of the National Bank Act in this form at once in order to prevent such conflicts of interest from happening again. Furthermore, Mr. Federal Chancellor should break his head on whether the National Bank would not be better served if the excellent economic personality of Commercial Councillor Schimetschek were a simple General Council member and the position of Vice-President would be perceived by someone else. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion, I don't want to go that far now; or should I perhaps? – ask the question in the room whether this order may not have something to do with the red-black "color teaching".
